76:["$","li","Federal side of premium changes ($0.0B)",{"children":[["$","span",null,{"className":"font-medium","children":["Federal side of premium changes ($0.0B)",":"]}]," ",["$","span",null,{"className":"text-foreground/80","children":"Private premiums change by −$274.7B for people insured today (+$32.0B of it from new benefits and the copay cap, with their overhead). Two federal costs follow premiums. People with premium tax credits (8.6% of the privately insured) pay a premium capped by income, so the government pays every dollar of a change. Employer premiums (81.4% of the privately insured) are untaxed: when they fall, 85.0% comes back as taxable wages, taxed at 33.3% (income tax and both halves of payroll tax), and when they rise the reverse. Together: 31.6% of every premium dollar, so the new benefits and the copay cap cost the government $10.1B and the premium savings return $97.0B."}]]}]

80:["$","li","Federal side of premium changes ($0.0B)",{"children":[["$","span",null,{"className":"font-medium","children":["Federal side of premium changes ($0.0B)",":"]}]," ",["$","span",null,{"className":"text-foreground/80","children":"Private premiums change by −$103.3B for people insured today (+$101.7B of it from new benefits and the copay cap, with their overhead). Two federal costs follow premiums. People with premium tax credits (8.6% of the privately insured) pay a premium capped by income, so the government pays every dollar of a change. Employer premiums (81.4% of the privately insured) are untaxed: when they fall, 85.0% comes back as taxable wages, taxed at 33.3% (income tax and both halves of payroll tax), and when they rise the reverse.
